You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **papers**:\n    27→  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n    28→  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n    29→  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n    30→- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    31→- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n    32→- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   175→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   176→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   177→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   178→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   179→\n   180→### Consistency requirements\n   181→\n   182→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   183→- Loading of data\n   184→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   185→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   186→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   187→\n   188→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   189→\n   190→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   191→\n   192→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   193→\n   194→Carefully verify your work after every step. Experimental and behavioral variables\n   300→  - Data processing\n   301→  - Data curation\n   302→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   303→\n   304→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   305→\n   306→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   307→\n   308→---\n   309→\n   310→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   311→\n   312→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   313→\n   314→**Actions**:\n   315→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   316→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   317→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   318→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   319→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   320→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   321→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   322→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   323→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   324→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   325→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   326→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   327→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   328→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   329→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   330→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   331→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   332→\n   333→**Done when**:\n   334→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   335→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   336→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   337→\n   338→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   339→\n   340→---\n   341→\n   342→### Step 6: Script Development\n   343→\n   344→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   345→\n   346→**Actions**:\n   347→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   348→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   349→- Include options:\n   350→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   351→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   352→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   353→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   354→- Write efficient code:\n   355→  - Vectorize loops\n   356→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   357→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   358→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   359→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   360→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   361→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   362→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   363→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   364→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   365→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   366→\n   367→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   368→\n   369→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   370→\n   371→---\n   372→\n   373→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   374→\n   375→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   376→\n   377→**Actions**:\n   378→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   379→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   380→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   381→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   382→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   383→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   384→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   385→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   386→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   387→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   388→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   389→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   390→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   391→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   392→\n   393→**Done when**: \n   394→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   395→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   396→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   397→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   398→\n   399→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   400→\n   401→---\n   402→\n   403→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   404→\n   405→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   406→\n   407→**Actions**:\n   408→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   409→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   410→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   411→\n   412→**Investigate any issues**:\n   413→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   414→\n   415→**Done when**: \n   416→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   417→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   418→- Accuracies are above chance\n   419→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   420→\n   421→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   422→\n   423→---\n   424→\n   425→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   426→\n   427→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   428→\n   429→**Actions**:\n   430→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   431→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   432→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   433→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   434→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   435→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   436→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   437→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   438→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   439→\n   440→**Done when**:\n   441→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   442→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   443→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   444→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   445→\n   446→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   447→\n   448→---\n   449→\n   450→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   451→\n   452→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   453→\n   454→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   455→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   456→\n   457→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   458→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   459→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   460→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   461→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   462→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   463→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   464→\n   465→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   466→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   467→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   468→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   469→- Fix any mismatches. \n   470→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   471→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   472→\n   473→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   474→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n   475→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   476→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   477→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   478→\n   479→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   480→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   481→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   482→\n   483→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   484→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   485→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   486→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   487→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   488→\n   489→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   490→\n   491→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   492→\n   493→---\n   494→\n   495→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   496→\n   497→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   498→\n   499→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   500→\n   501→**Actions**:\n   502→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   503→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   504→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   505→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n   506→\n   507→**Done when**: \n   508→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   509→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   510→\n   511→---\n   512→\n   513→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   514→\n   515→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   516→\n   517→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   518→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   519→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   520→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   521→\n   522→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n   523→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n   524→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers's accuracy for each variable\n   525→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   526→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   527→\n   528→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   529→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   530→\n   531→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   532→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   533→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   534→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   535→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   536→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n   537→\n   538→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   539→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   540→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   541→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   542→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   543→\n   544→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   545→\n   546→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   547→\n   548→---\n   549→\n   550→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   551→\n   552→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   553→\n   554→**Actions**:\n   555→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   556→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   557→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   558→  - Validation results and tables\n   559→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   560→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   561→  - Dataset description\n   562→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   563→  - Output format specification\n   564→  - Key statistics\n   565→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   566→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   567→\n   568→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   569→\n   570→---\n   571→\n   572→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   573→\n   574→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   826→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   827→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   828→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   829→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   830→\n   831→### Computational Efficiency\n   832→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   833→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   834→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   835→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   836→\n   837→## Success Criteria\n   838→\n   839→A successful conversion should:\n   840→1. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. The reference code uses SessionLoader.load_wheel() which interpolates to 1kHz\n   246→    # and computes velocity via Gaussian smoothing. We replicate this:\n   247→    # Interpolate position to uniform 1kHz sampling\n   248→    dt = 0.001  # 1kHz\n   249→    t_uniform = np.arange(wh_times[0], wh_times[-1], dt)\n   250→    pos_interp = np.interp(t_uniform, wh_times, wh_pos)\n   251→\n   252→    # Compute velocity via finite differences (matching brainbox wheel processing)\n   253→    velocity = np.gradient(pos_interp, dt)\n   254→    speed = np.abs(velocity)\n   255→\n   256→    return t_uniform, speed\n   257→\n   258→\n   259→def load_whisker_me(sdir):\n   260→    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy. Fall back to right camera\n   285→    right_me_files, right_time_files = _find_files(\n   286→        sdir, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy', '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n   287→\n   288→    if right_me_files and right_time_files:\n   289→        me = np.load(right_me_files[-1]).flatten()\n   290→        times = np.load(right_time_files[-1]).flatten()\n   291→        min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n   292→        return times[:min_len], me[:min_len]\n   293→\n   294→    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No whisker motion energy data found in {sdir}\")\n   295→\n   296→\n   297→def interpolate_behavior_to_bins(beh_times, beh_vals, interval_begs, interval_ends):\n   298→    \"\"\"Interpolate behavioral signal to trial time bins.\n   299→\n   300→    Matches reference code get_behavior_per_interval:\n   301→    - x_interp = linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)\n   302→    - Interpolate using linear interp1d with extrapolation\n   303→\n   304→    Returns:\n   305→        values: array (n_trials, n_bins)\n   306→        mask: boolean array (n_trials,) - True for good trials\n   307→    \"\"\"\n   308→    n_trials = len(interval_begs)\n   309→    values = np.full((n_trials, N_BINS), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   310→    mask = np.ones(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n   311→\n   312→    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n   313→        t_beg = interval_begs[trial_idx]\n   314→        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n   315→\n   316→        if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\n   317→            mask[trial_idx] = False\n   318→            continue\n   319→\n   320→        # Find behavior samples in this interval\n   321→        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_beg, side='right')\n   322→        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n   323→\n   324→        beh_t = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   325→        beh_v = beh_vals[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   326→\n   327→        if len(beh_v) == 0:\n   328→            mask[trial_idx] = False\n   329→            continue\n   330→\n   331→        # Check coverage (matching reference code)\n   332→        if np.abs(t_beg - beh_t[0]) > BINSIZE:\n   333→            mask[trial_idx] = False\n   334→            continue\n   335→        if np.abs(t_end - beh_t[-1]) > BINSIZE:\n   336→            mask[trial_idx] = False\n   337→            continue\n   338→\n   339→        # Interpolation points matching reference: linspace(beg + binsize, end, n_bins)\n   340→        x_interp = np.linspace(t_beg + BINSIZE, t_end, N_BINS)\n   341→\n   342→        try:\n   343→            interp_func = interp1d(beh_t, beh_v, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')\n   344→            values[trial_idx] = interp_func(x_interp).astype(np.float32)\n   345→        except Exception:\n   346→            mask[trial_idx] = False\n   347→\n   348→    return values, mask\n   349→\n   350→\n   351→def compute_trial_num_in_block(prob_left):\n   352→    \"\"\"Compute trial number within block.\n   353→\n   354→    A block is a contiguous sequence of trials with the same probabilityLeft.\n   355→    Trial number resets to 1 at each block boundary.\n   356→    \"\"\"\n   357→    trial_nums = np.ones(len(prob_left), dtype=np.int32)\n   358→    for i in range(1, len(prob_left)):\n   359→        if prob_left[i] == prob_left[i - 1]:\n   360→            trial_nums[i] = trial_nums[i - 1] + 1\n   361→        else:\n   362→            trial_nums[i] = 1\n   363→    return trial_nums\n   364→\n   365→\n   366→def discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=3):\n   367→    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins categories using quantiles.\n   368→\n   369→    Computes quantile boundaries from non-NaN values and assigns each value\n   370→    to a bin. Returns integer bin indices.\n   371→    \"\"\"\n   372→    flat = values[~np.isnan(values)].flatten()\n   373→    if len(flat) == 0:\n   374→        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=np.int32)\n   375→\n   376→    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)[1:-1]\n   377→    boundaries = np.percentile(flat, quantiles)\n   378→\n   379→    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries).astype(np.int32)\n   380→    return result\n   381→\n   382→\n   383→def process_session(sdir, br, show_processing=False, session_idx=0):\n   384→    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\n   385→\n   386→    Returns None if session cannot be processed.\n   387→    \"\"\"\n   388→    lab, subject, date = parse_session_info(sdir)\n   389→    session_id = f\"{subject}_{date}\"\n   390→    t0 = time.time()\n   391→\n   392→    try:\n   393→        # 1. Load trials\n   394→        trials = load_trials(sdir)\n   395→        mask = create_trial_mask(trials)\n   396→\n   397→        # 2. Load spikes\n   398→        spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions = load_spikes(sdir)\n   399→        n_clusters = len(cluster_regions)\n   400→\n   401→        # 3. Compute trial intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n   402→        stim_on = trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n   403→        interval_begs = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n   404→        interval_ends = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n   405→\n   406→        # 4. Bin spikes for ALL trials first (before masking)\n   407→        print(f\"  Binning spikes ({n_clusters} neurons, {len(trials)} trials)...\", flush=True)\n   408→        t_bin = time.time()\n   409→        binned_spikes = bin_spikes_vectorized(\n   410→            spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends\n   411→        )\n   412→        print(f\"  Spike binning: {time.time() - t_bin:.1f}s\", flush=True)\n   413→\n   414→        # 5. Load and bin behavioral data\n   415→        print(f\"  Loading wheel speed...\", flush=True)\n   416→        wh_times, wh_speed = load_wheel_speed(sdir)\n   417→        wheel_vals, wheel_mask = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n   418→            wh_times, wh_speed, interval_begs, interval_ends\n   419→        )\n   420→\n   421→        print(f\"  Loading whisker ME...\", flush=True)\n   422→        me_times, me_vals_raw = load_whisker_me(sdir)\n   423→        whisker_vals, whisker_mask = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n   424→            me_times, me_vals_raw, interval_begs, interval_ends\n   425→        )\n   426→\n   427→        # 6. Combine masks: trial quality + behavior availability\n   428→        # Following reference code align_spike_behavior\n   429→        combined_mask = mask.values & wheel_mask & whisker_mask\n   430→\n   431→        # Apply mask\n   432→        good_indices = np.where(combined_mask)[0]\n   433→        if len(good_indices) < 2:\n   434→            print(f\"  WARNING: Only {len(good_indices)} valid trials, skipping session\", flush=True)\n   435→            return None\n   436→\n   437→        neural_trials = binned_spikes[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n   438→        wheel_trials = wheel_vals[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n   439→        whisker_trials = whisker_vals[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n   440→\n   441→        # 7. Extract per-trial variables\n   442→        trials_good = trials.iloc[good_indices]\n   443→\n   444→        # Choice: -1 (left) -> 0, 1 (right) -> 1\n   445→        choice = trials_good['choice'].values.copy()\n   446→        choice_binary = np.where(choice == 1, 1, 0).astype(np.int32)\n   447→\n   448→        # Prior: probabilityLeft -> 0.2->0, 0.5->1, 0.8->2\n   449→        prob_left = trials_good['probabilityLeft'].values\n   450→        prior = np.full(len(prob_left), 1, dtype=np.int32)  # default 0.5->1\n   451→        prior[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2, atol=0.05)] = 0\n   452→        prior[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8, atol=0.05)] = 2\n   453→\n   454→        # Trial number in block\n   455→        trial_num_in_block = compute_trial_num_in_block(prob_left)\n   456→\n   457→        # 8. Discretize continuous outputs\n   458→        wheel_discrete = discretize_to_bins(wheel_trials, n_bins=3)\n   459→        whisker_discrete = discretize_to_bins(whisker_trials, n_bins=3)\n   460→\n   461→        # 9. Build time input\n   462→        # Time since stimulus onset: center of each bin\n   463→        time_input = np.linspace(\n   464→            TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE / 2,\n   465→            TIME_WINDOW[1] - BINSIZE / 2,\n   466→            N_BINS\n   467→        ).astype(np.float32)\n   468→\n   469→        # 10. Format as lists of per-trial arrays\n   470→        n_trials = len(good_indices)\n   471→\n   472→        # Store as uint8 during accumulation to save memory (spike counts rarely exceed 255)\n   473→        # Will be converted to float32 when decoder loads it\n   474→        neural_list = [np.clip(neural_trials[i], 0, 255).astype(np.uint8) for i in range(n_trials)]\n   475→\n   476→        input_list = []\n   477→        for i in range(n_trials):\n   478→            inp = np.stack([\n   479→                time_input,\n   480→                np.full(N_BINS, trial_num_in_block[i], dtype=np.float32)\n   481→            ], axis=0)  # (2, 100)\n   482→            input_list.append(inp)\n   483→\n   484→        output_list = []\n   485→        for i in range(n_trials):\n   486→            out = np.stack([\n   487→                np.full(N_BINS, choice_binary[i], dtype=np.int64),\n   488→                np.full(N_BINS, prior[i], dtype=np.int64),\n   489→                wheel_discrete[i].astype(np.int64),\n   490→                whisker_discrete[i].astype(np.int64)\n   491→            ], axis=0)  # (4, 100)\n   492→            output_list.append(out)\n   493→\n   494→        elapsed = time.time() - t0\n   495→        print(f\"  Session {session_id}: {n_trials} trials, {n_clusters} neurons, {elapsed:.1f}s\", flush=True)\n   496→\n   497→        # Show processing plots if requested\n   498→        if show_processing:\n   499→            plot_processing(\n   500→                session_id, trials_good, neural_trials, wheel_trials, whisker_trials,\n   501→                choice_binary, prior, wheel_discrete, whisker_discrete,\n   502→                time_input, interval_begs[good_indices], interval_ends[good_indices],\n   503→                session_idx\n   504→            )\n   505→\n   506→        return {\n   507→            'neural': neural_list,\n   508→            'input': input_list,\n   509→            'output': output_list,\n   510→            'subject': subject,\n   511→            'cluster_regions': cluster_regions,\n   512→            'n_trials': n_trials,\n   513→            'n_neurons': n_clusters,\n   514→            'session_id': session_id,\n   515→        }\n   516→\n   517→    except Exception as e:\n   518→        print(f\"  ERROR processing {session_id}: {e}\", flush=True)\n   519→        import traceback\n   520→        traceback.print_exc()\n   521→        return None\n   522→\n   523→\n   524→def plot_processing(session_id, trials, neural, wheel, whisker,\n   525→                    choice, prior, wheel_disc, whisker_disc,\n   526→                    time_input, interval_begs, interval_ends, session_idx):\n   527→    \"\"\"Plot processing visualizations for a session.\"\"\"\n   528→    import matplotlib\n   529→    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n   530→    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n   531→\n   532→    n_trials_plot = min(3, len(trials))\n   533→    fig, axes = plt.subplots(6, n_trials_plot, figsize=(5 * n_trials_plot, 20))\n   534→    if n_trials_plot == 1:\n   535→        axes = axes[:, np.newaxis]\n   536→\n   537→    for i in range(n_trials_plot):\n   538→        t = time_input\n   539→\n   540→        # 2s trial\nBINSIZE = 0.02  # 20ms\nINTERVAL_LEN = TIME_WINDOW[1] - TIME_WINDOW[0]  # 2.0s\nN_BINS = int(np.ceil(INTERVAL_LEN / BINSIZE))  # 100\n\nMIN_RT = 0.08\nMAX_RT = 2.0\nMAX_TRIAL_LEN = 10.0\n\nNAN_EXCLUDE = [\n    'stimOn_times', 'choice', 'feedback_times',\n    'probabilityLeft', 'firstMovement_times', 'feedbackType'\n]\n\nDATA_ROOT = Path('data/one_cache')\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Helper functions\n# ============================================================\n\ndef find_session_dirs():\n    \"\"\"Find all session directories with required data.\"\"\"\n    session_dirs = sorted(glob.glob(str(DATA_ROOT / '*/Subjects/*/*/001')))\n    valid = []\n    for sdir in session_dirs:\n        has_spikes = len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/probe*/pykilosort/*/spikes.times.npy'))) > 0\n        has_trials = len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/_ibl_trials.table.pqt'))) > 0\n        has_wheel = os.path.exists(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy'))\n        # Check for ME files in dated subdirectories (alf/#date#/)\n        has_me = (len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy'))) > 0 or\n                  len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy'))) > 0)\n        # Check for camera times in alf/ or alf/#date#/\n        has_me_times = (os.path.exists(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')) or\n                       os.path.exists(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')) or\n                       len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy'))) > 0 or\n                       len(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy'))) > 0)\n        has_me = has_me and has_me_times\n        if has_spikes and has_trials and has_wheel and has_me:\n            valid.append(sdir)\n    return valid\n\n\ndef parse_session_info(sdir):\n    \"\"\"Extract lab, subject, date from session directory path.\"\"\"\n    parts = Path(sdir).parts\n    sub_idx = parts.index('Subjects')\n    lab = parts[sub_idx - 1]\n    subject = parts[sub_idx + 1]\n    date = parts[sub_idx + 2]\n    return lab, subject, date\n\n\ndef load_trials(sdir):\n    \"\"\"Load trials table from parquet file.\"\"\"\n    trial_files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/*/_ibl_trials.table.pqt')))\n    if not trial_files:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No trials table found in {sdir}\")\n    # Use the most recent revision\n    trials = pd.read_parquet(trial_files[-1])\n    return trials\n\n\ndef create_trial_mask(trials):\n    \"\"\"Create trial mask following reference code load_trials_and_mask.\n\n    Excludes:\n    - RT < 0.08 or RT > 2.0\n    - No-choice trials (choice == 0)\n    - Trials with NaN in key events\n    - Trial length > 10s\n    \"\"\"\n    mask = pd.Series(True, index=trials.index)\n\n    # RT filter\n    rt = trials['firstMovement_times'] - trials['stimOn_times']\n    mask &= (rt >= MIN_RT) & (rt <= MAX_RT)\n\n    # Trial length filter (goCue to feedback)\n    if 'goCue_times' in trials.columns and 'feedback_times' in trials.columns:\n        trial_len = trials['feedback_times'] - trials['goCue_times']\n        mask &= (trial_len <= MAX_TRIAL_LEN) | trial_len.isna()\n\n    # No-choice exclusion\n    mask &= (trials['choice'] != 0)\n\n    # NaN exclusion\n    for col in NAN_EXCLUDE:\n        if col in trials.columns:\n            mask &= ~trials[col].isna()\n\n    return mask\n\n\ndef load_spikes(sdir):\n    \"\"\"Load and merge spikes from all probes in a session.\n\n    Following reference code: no QC filtering (qc=None).\n    \"\"\"\n    probe_dirs = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/probe*/pykilosort/*')))\n\n    all_spike_times = []\n    all_spike_clusters = []\n    all_cluster_regions = []\n    cluster_offset = 0\n\n    br = BrainRegions()\n\n    for pdir in probe_dirs:\n        st_file = os.path.join(pdir, 'spikes.times.npy')\n        sc_file = os.path.join(pdir, 'spikes.clusters.npy')\n        cc_file = os.path.join(pdir, 'clusters.channels.npy')\n        cb_file = os.path.join(pdir, 'channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy')\n\n        if not all(os.path.exists(f) for f in [st_file, sc_file, cc_file, cb_file]):\n            continue\n\n        spike_times = np.load(st_file).flatten()\n        spike_clusters = np.load(sc_file).flatten()\n        cluster_channels = np.load(cc_file).flatten()\n        channel_brain_ids = np.load(cb_file).flatten()\n\n        n_clusters = len(cluster_channels)\n\n        # Map clusters to brain regions\n        # Clip channel indices to valid range\n        valid_channels = np.clip(cluster_channels, 0, len(channel_brain_ids) - 1)\n        cluster_brain_ids = channel_brain_ids[valid_channels]\n        cluster_acronyms = br.id2acronym(cluster_brain_ids)\n        beryl_regions = br.acronym2acronym(cluster_acronyms, mapping='Beryl')\n\n        # Offset cluster IDs for merging\n        spike_clusters_offset = spike_clusters + cluster_offset\n        cluster_offset += n_clusters\n\n        all_spike_times.append(spike_times)\n        all_spike_clusters.append(spike_clusters_offset)\n        all_cluster_regions.extend(beryl_regions)\n\n    if not all_spike_times:\n        raise ValueError(f\"No spike data found in {sdir}\")\n\n    # Merge and sort by time\n    merged_times = np.concatenate(all_spike_times)\n    del all_spike_times\n    merged_clusters = np.concatenate(all_spike_clusters)\n    del all_spike_clusters\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(merged_times, kind='stable')\n    merged_times = merged_times[sort_idx]\n    merged_clusters = merged_clusters[sort_idx].astype(np.int32)\n    del sort_idx\n\n    return merged_times, merged_clusters, np.array(all_cluster_regions)\n\n\ndef bin_spikes_vectorized(spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends):\n    \"\"\"Bin spikes into time bins for all trials.\n\n    Returns array of shape (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins).\n    Matches reference code bin_spiking_data / get_spike_data_per_interval.\n\n    Uses searchsorted for fast trial assignment and flat indexing for vectorized counting.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_begs)\n    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    # Process each trial using searchsorted for fast spike selection\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_beg = interval_begs[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            continue\n\n        # Use searchsorted on sorted spike_times for O(log n) lookup\n        i_start = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_beg, side='left')\n        i_end = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        if i_start >= i_end:\n            continue\n\n        times_trial = spike_times[i_start:i_end]\n        clusters_trial = spike_clusters[i_start:i_end]\n\n        # Compute bin indices for each spike\n        bin_idx = np.minimum(\n            ((times_trial - t_beg) / BINSIZE).astype(np.int32),\n            N_BINS - 1\n        )\n\n        # Use flat index: (cluster * N_BINS + bin) and bincount\n        flat_idx = clusters_trial * N_BINS + bin_idx\n        counts = np.bincount(flat_idx, minlength=n_clusters * N_BINS)\n        binned[trial_idx] = counts[:n_clusters * N_BINS].reshape(n_clusters, N_BINS)\n\n    return binned\n\n\ndef load_wheel_speed(sdir):\n    \"\"\"Load wheel speed (absolute velocity) following reference code.\n\n    Reference: load_target_behavior for 'wheel-speed' uses SessionLoader.load_wheel()\n    which returns interpolated wheel with velocity computed via Gaussian smoothing.\n    We need to replicate this from raw position + timestamps.\n    \"\"\"\n    wh_pos = np.load(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/_ibl_wheel.position.npy')).flatten()\n    wh_times = np.load(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf/_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy')).flatten()\n\n    # The reference code uses SessionLoader.load_wheel() which interpolates to 1kHz\n    # and computes velocity via Gaussian smoothing. We replicate this:\n    # Interpolate position to uniform 1kHz sampling\n    dt = 0.001  # 1kHz\n    t_uniform = np.arange(wh_times[0], wh_times[-1], dt)\n    pos_interp = np.interp(t_uniform, wh_times, wh_pos)\n\n    # Compute velocity via finite differences (matching brainbox wheel processing)\n    velocity = np.gradient(pos_interp, dt)\n    speed = np.abs(velocity)\n\n    return t_uniform, speed\n\n\ndef load_whisker_me(sdir):\n    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy. Try left camera first, then right.\n\n    Matches reference code bin_behaviors / load_target_behavior for 'whisker-motion-energy'.\n    \"\"\"\n    def _find_files(sdir, me_pattern, time_pattern):\n        \"\"\"Search for ME and time files in dated subdirs (alf/#date#/) and alf/ root.\"\"\"\n        me_files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf', '*', me_pattern)))\n        time_files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sdir, 'alf', '*', time_pattern)))\n        # Also check directly in alf/\n        direct_time = os.path.join(sdir, 'alf', time_pattern)\n        if os.path.exists(direct_time) and direct_time not in time_files:\n            time_files.append(direct_time)\n        return me_files, sorted(time_files)\n\n    # Try left camera first (matching reference code)\n    left_me_files, left_time_files = _find_files(\n        sdir, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy', '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n\n    if left_me_files and left_time_files:\n        me = np.load(left_me_files[-1]).flatten()\n        times = np.load(left_time_files[-1]).flatten()\n        min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n        return times[:min_len], me[:min_len]\n\n    # Fall back to right camera\n    right_me_files, right_time_files = _find_files(\n        sdir, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy', '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n\n    if right_me_files and right_time_files:\n        me = np.load(right_me_files[-1]).flatten()\n        times = np.load(right_time_files[-1]).flatten()\n        min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n        return times[:min_len], me[:min_len]\n\n    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No whisker motion energy data found in {sdir}\")\n\n\ndef interpolate_behavior_to_bins(beh_times, beh_vals, interval_begs, interval_ends):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate behavioral signal to trial time bins.\n\n    Matches reference code get_behavior_per_interval:\n    - x_interp = linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)\n    - Interpolate using linear interp1d with extrapolation\n\n    Returns:\n        values: array (n_trials, n_bins)\n        mask: boolean array (n_trials,) - True for good trials\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_begs)\n    values = np.full((n_trials, N_BINS), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    mask = np.ones(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_beg = interval_begs[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            mask[trial_idx] = False\n            continue\n\n        # Find behavior samples in this interval\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_beg, side='right')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        beh_t = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        beh_v = beh_vals[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        if len(beh_v) == 0:\n            mask[trial_idx] = False\n            continue\n\n        # Check coverage (matching reference code)\n        if np.abs(t_beg - beh_t[0]) > BINSIZE:\n            mask[trial_idx] = False\n            continue\n        if np.abs(t_end - beh_t[-1]) > BINSIZE:\n            mask[trial_idx] = False\n            continue\n\n        # Interpolation points matching reference: linspace(beg + binsize, end, n_bins)\n        x_interp = np.linspace(t_beg + BINSIZE, t_end, N_BINS)\n\n        try:\n            interp_func = interp1d(beh_t, beh_v, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')\n            values[trial_idx] = interp_func(x_interp).astype(np.float32)\n        except Exception:\n            mask[trial_idx] = False\n\n    return values, mask\n\n\ndef compute_trial_num_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"Compute trial number within block.\n\n    A block is a contiguous sequence of trials with the same probabilityLeft.\n    Trial number resets to 1 at each block boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n    trial_nums = np.ones(len(prob_left), dtype=np.int32)\n    for i in range(1, len(prob_left)):\n        if prob_left[i] == prob_left[i - 1]:\n            trial_nums[i] = trial_nums[i - 1] + 1\n        else:\n            trial_nums[i] = 1\n    return trial_nums\n\n\ndef discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=3):\n    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins categories using quantiles.\n\n    Computes quantile boundaries from non-NaN values and assigns each value\n    to a bin. Returns integer bin indices.\n    \"\"\"\n    flat = values[~np.isnan(values)].flatten()\n    if len(flat) == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=np.int32)\n\n    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)[1:-1]\n    boundaries = np.percentile(flat, quantiles)\n\n    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries).astype(np.int32)\n    return result\n\n\ndef process_session(sdir, br, show_processing=False, session_idx=0):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\n\n    Returns None if session cannot be processed.\n    \"\"\"\n    lab, subject, date = parse_session_info(sdir)\n    session_id = f\"{subject}_{date}\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    try:\n        # 1. Load trials\n        trials = load_trials(sdir)\n        mask = create_trial_mask(trials)\n\n        # 2. Load spikes\n        spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions = load_spikes(sdir)\n        n_clusters = len(cluster_regions)\n\n        # 3. Compute trial intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n        stim_on = trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n        interval_begs = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n        interval_ends = stim_on + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n\n        # 4. Bin spikes for ALL trials first (before masking)\n        print(f\"  Binning spikes ({n_clusters} neurons, {len(trials)} trials)...\", flush=True)\n        t_bin = time.time()\n        binned_spikes = bin_spikes_vectorized(\n            spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends\n        )\n        print(f\"  Spike binning: {time.time() - t_bin:.1f}s\", flush=True)\n\n        # 5. Load and bin behavioral data\n        print(f\"  Loading wheel speed...\", flush=True)\n        wh_times, wh_speed = load_wheel_speed(sdir)\n        wheel_vals, wheel_mask = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n            wh_times, wh_speed, interval_begs, interval_ends\n        )\n\n        print(f\"  Loading whisker ME...\", flush=True)\n        me_times, me_vals_raw = load_whisker_me(sdir)\n        whisker_vals, whisker_mask = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n            me_times, me_vals_raw, interval_begs, interval_ends\n        )\n\n        # 6. Combine masks: trial quality + behavior availability\n        # Following reference code align_spike_behavior\n        combined_mask = mask.values & wheel_mask & whisker_mask\n\n        # Apply mask\n        good_indices = np.where(combined_mask)[0]\n        if len(good_indices) < 2:\n            print(f\"  WARNING: Only {len(good_indices)} valid trials, skipping session\", flush=True)\n            return None\n\n        neural_trials = binned_spikes[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n        wheel_trials = wheel_vals[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n        whisker_trials = whisker_vals[good_indices]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n\n        # 7. Extract per-trial variables\n        trials_good = trials.iloc[good_indices]\n\n        # Choice: -1 (left) -> 0, 1 (right) -> 1\n        choice = trials_good['choice'].values.copy()\n        choice_binary = np.where(choice == 1, 1, 0).astype(np.int32)\n\n        # Prior: probabilityLeft -> 0.2->0, 0.5->1, 0.8->2\n        prob_left = trials_good['probabilityLeft'].values\n        prior = np.full(len(prob_left), 1, dtype=np.int32)  # default 0.5->1\n        prior[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2, atol=0.05)] = 0\n        prior[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8, atol=0.05)] = 2\n\n        # Trial number in block\n        trial_num_in_block = compute_trial_num_in_block(prob_left)\n\n        # 8. Discretize continuous outputs\n        wheel_discrete = discretize_to_bins(wheel_trials, n_bins=3)\n        whisker_discrete = discretize_to_bins(whisker_trials, n_bins=3)\n\n        # 9. Build time input\n        # Time since stimulus onset: center of each bin\n        time_input = np.linspace(\n            TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE / 2,\n            TIME_WINDOW[1] - BINSIZE / 2,\n            N_BINS\n        ).astype(np.float32)\n\n        # 10. Format as lists of per-trial arrays\n        n_trials = len(good_indices)\n\n        # Store as uint8 during accumulation to save memory (spike counts rarely exceed 255)\n        # Will be converted to float32 when decoder loads it\n        neural_list = [np.clip(neural_trials[i], 0, 255).astype(np.uint8) for i in range(n_trials)]\n\n        input_list = []\n        for i in range(n_trials):\n            inp = np.stack([\n                time_input,\n                np.full(N_BINS, trial_num_in_block[i], dtype=np.float32)\n            ], axis=0)  # (2, 100)\n            input_list.append(inp)\n\n        output_list = []\n        for i in range(n_trials):\n            out = np.stack([\n                np.full(N_BINS, choice_binary[i], dtype=np.int64),\n                np.full(N_BINS, prior[i], dtype=np.int64),\n                wheel_discrete[i].astype(np.int64),\n                whisker_discrete[i].astype(np.int64)\n            ], axis=0)  # (4, 100)\n            output_list.append(out)\n\n        elapsed = time.time() - t0\n        print(f\"  Session {session_id}: {n_trials} trials, {n_clusters} neurons, {elapsed:.1f}s\", flush=True)\n\n        # Show processing plots if requested\n        if show_processing:\n            plot_processing(\n                session_id, trials_good, neural_trials, wheel_trials, whisker_trials,\n                choice_binary, prior, wheel_discrete, whisker_discrete,\n                time_input, interval_begs[good_indices], interval_ends[good_indices],\n                session_idx\n            )\n\n        return {\n            'neural': neural_list,\n            'input': input_list,\n            'output': output_list,\n            'subject': subject,\n            'cluster_regions': cluster_regions,\n            'n_trials': n_trials,\n            'n_neurons': n_clusters,\n            'session_id': session_id,\n        }\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  ERROR processing {session_id}: {e}\", flush=True)\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc()\n        return None\n\n\ndef plot_processing(session_id, trials, neural, wheel, whisker,\n                    choice, prior, wheel_disc, whisker_disc,\n                    time_input, interval_begs, interval_ends, session_idx):\n    \"\"\"Plot processing visualizations for a session.\"\"\"\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    n_trials_plot = min(3, len(trials))\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(6, n_trials_plot, figsize=(5 * n_trials_plot, 20))\n    if n_trials_plot == 1:\n        axes = axes[:, np.newaxis]\n\n    for i in range(n_trials_plot):\n        t = time_input\n\n        # Neural activity (mean across neurons)\n        ax = axes[0, i]\n        mean_rate = neural[i].mean(axis=0)\n        ax.plot(t, mean_rate)\n        ax.set_title(f'Trial {i}: Mean firing rate')\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5, label='stim onset')\n        ax.set_ylabel('Spike count')\n\n        # Wheel speed continuous\n        ax = axes[1, i]\n        ax.plot(t, wheel[i])\n        ax.set_title(f'Wheel speed (continuous)')\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n\n        # Wheel speed discretized\n        ax = axes[2, i]\n        ax.plot(t, wheel_disc[i])\n        ax.set_title(f'Wheel speed (discretized)')\n        ax.set_yticks([0, 1, 2])\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n\n        # Whisker ME continuous\n        ax = axes[3, i]\n        ax.plot(t, whisker[i])\n        ax.set_title(f'Whisker ME (continuous)')\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n\n        # Whisker ME discretized\n        ax = axes[4, i]\n        ax.plot(t, whisker_disc[i])\n        ax.set_title(f'Whisker ME (discretized)')\n        ax.set_yticks([0, 1, 2])\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n\n        # `single_region`: False (use all regions)\n- Behaviors: choice, reward, block, wheel-speed, whisker-motion-energy\n\n**Trial filtering (`load_trials_and_mask`):**\n- min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0 (reaction stimOn_times, window (-0.6, -0.1)s\n- Wheel speed, whisker ME: align to firstMovement_times, window (0, 1)s, 20ms bins\n\n**BUT**: The reference code (`0_data_caching.py`) uses stimOn_times alignment with (-0.5, 1.5) window and 20ms bins for ALL behaviors. The decoder task instructions say \"Temporally align based on stimulus onset\" - so we follow the code + decoder task.\n\n**Neuron curation (from data paper):**\n- Paper: amplitude > 50uV, noise cutoff < 20uV, refractory period violation -> \"well-isolated\"\n- Code: qc=None -> uses ALL neurons (not just good ones)\n- We follow the code: use ALL neurons\n\n**Trial curation:**\n- RT between 0.08-2.0s\n- Exclude no-choice trials (choice==0)\n- Exclude trials with NaN in key events\n- max_trial_len=10.0\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**: Use ALL neurons (qc=None), matching reference code.\n\n**Trial curation rules**: Apply `load_trials_and_mask` logic - exclude bad RT, no-choice, NaN trials.\n\n### Decoders Trained (from papers)\n| Decoded variable | Metric | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------|-------|\n| Choice | AUC/balanced accuracy | Binary (left/right) |\n| Prior | AUC/balanced accuracy | Categorical |\n| Wheel speed | R^2 | Continuous -> discretize for our decoder |\n| Whisker motion energy | R^2 | Continuous -> discretize for our decoder |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Sessions | Uses bwm_df to get eids | 409 sessions on disk | 459 released, 433 used | Use all 393 sessions with complete data |\n| Neurons | qc=None (all neurons) | 555k total, 68k good | 621k total, 75k good | Code uses ALL neurons. Disk has fewer because only ~409 sessions |\n| Alignment | stimOn_times, (-0.5, 1.5) | N/A | Choice/prior=stimOn, wheel/whisker=firstMovement | Follow code + decoder task: stimOn_times for all |\n| Binsize | 0.02 (20ms) | N/A | 20ms for wheel/whisker, 50ms for choice/prior | Code uses 20ms uniformly. terciles\n- `whisker_motion_energy`: shape (1, 100) per trial, discretized into 3 bins (0/1/2) using terciles\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Alignment**: stimOn_times for all, matching code + decoder task instructions\n2. **Binsize**: 20ms uniformly, matching code\n3. **All neurons**: No QC filtering, matching code (qc=None)\n4. **Whisker ME source**: Try left camera first, fall back to right, matching code\n5. **Discretization**: Use session-wide terciles for wheel speed and whisker ME\n6. **Block trial number**: Computed as position within contiguous block of same probabilityLeft\n\n### Planned specified interval in each recording.\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    times : array-like\\n        time in seconds for each spike\\n    clusters : array-like\\n        cluster id for each work with nested functions\\n    @globalize\\n    def compute_spike_count(interval):\\n        # We use interval_idx to track the interval order while working with p.imap_unordered()\\n        interval_idx, t_beg, t_end = interval\\n        idxs_t = (times >= t_beg) & (times < t_end)\\n        times_curr = times[idxs_t]\\n        clust_curr = clusters[idxs_t]\\n        if times_curr.shape[0] == 0:\\n            # no spikes in this trial\\n            binned_spikes_tmp = np.zeros((n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\\n            if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\\n                t_idxs = np.nan * np.ones(n_bins)\\n            else:\\n                t_idxs = np.arange(t_beg, t_end + binsize / 2, binsize)\\n            idxs_tmp = np.arange(n_clusters_in_region)\\n (window_start, window_end), relative to align_time\\n        binsize : float, optional\\n            size of bins in seconds for multi-bin decoding\\n            \\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n interval_idx, t_beg, t_end = interval\\n        idxs_t = (times >= t_beg) & (times < t_end)\\n        times_curr = times[idxs_t]\\n        clust_curr = clusters[idxs_t]\\n        if times_curr.shape[0] == 0:\\n            # no spikes in this trial\\n            binned_spikes_tmp = np.zeros((n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\\n            if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\\n                t_idxs = np.nan * np.ones(n_bins)\\n            else:\\n                t_idxs = np.arange(t_beg, t_end + binsize / 2, binsize)\\n            idxs_tmp = np.arange(n_clusters_in_region)\\n (window_start, window_end), relative to align_time\\n        binsize : float, optional\\n            size of bins in seconds for multi-bin decoding\\n            \\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    list\\n        each element is a 2D numpy.ndarray for a single interval of shape (n_bins, n_clusters)\\n    array\\n        cluster ids that account for axis 1 of the above 2D arrays.\\n
